H:=L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),\mathcal{D}),\quad H^{1,2}:=H^{1,2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),W_{2},\mathcal{D}).

The connection between the particle system (
4.1
) and the PDE (
3.10
) is established through an appropriate Kolmogorov formula, stated in Theorem
4.19
below. This representation is obtained by expanding the solution to (
3.10
) along the particle system (
4.1
) via a suitable chain rule. The proof of this expansion relies on Proposition
2.18
and first requires approximating the solution to the PDE (
3.2
), when driven by arbitrary boundary and source terms, by solutions to the same PDE but with cylindrical coefficients. As clarified in Proposition
4.15
, a key step is to show that, for cylindrical coefficients, the solution to (
3.2
) itself inherits a form of cylindrical structure which we make precise below.

Definition 4.13
.

We say that a function
u:\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
is
fiber-wise cylinder
if there exists
(u^{\mathbf{s}})_{\mathbf{s}\in T_{o}^{\infty}}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\infty}_{c}
such that:

1)

u(\mu)=u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}(\mu)
for every
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, where
\mathbf{s}(\mu)
is as in (
2.5
);

2)

setting

(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u)_{\mu}:=(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)})_{\mu},\quad(L_{c}u)_{\mu}:=(L_{c}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)})_{\mu},\quad\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

we have that
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u\in L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\overline{{\mathcal{D}}};\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
u,L_{c}u\in H
.

We denote the space of fiber-wise cylinder function with
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
.

Note that the definition of
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
and
L_{c}u
are well posed since, for a every
\mathbf{s}\in T_{o}^{\infty}
,
u^{\mathbf{s}}
belongs to
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
which is included in the domain of both the operators
\boldsymbol{\nabla}
and
L_{c}
, see (
2.16
) and Lemma
2.8
.
Also, clearly,
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
and the definitions of
\boldsymbol{\nabla}
and
L_{c}
are consistent.

Proposition 4.14
.

We have that
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}\subset D(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and

(4.21)

{\mathrm{D}}u=\boldsymbol{\nabla}u,\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}u=L_{c}u\quad\text{ for every }u\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}.

We stress that the statement above means that, for every
u\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
, the
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),{\mathcal{D}})
-equivalence class represented by the
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e. defined function

u
belongs to the domain of the operator
\boldsymbol{\Delta}
(and thus, in particular, also to the domain of
{\mathrm{D}}
) and the
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\overline{{\mathcal{D}}};\mathbb{R}^{d})
function
{\mathrm{D}}u
can be represented by the
\overline{{\mathcal{D}}}
-a.e. defined function
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
(respectively, the
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),{\mathcal{D}})
function
\boldsymbol{\Delta}u
can be represented by the
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e. defined function
L_{c}u
) as in Definition
4.13
.

Proof.

We claim that the statement follows if we show that

(4.22)

(L_{c}u,w)_{H}=-\langle\boldsymbol{\nabla}u,\boldsymbol{\nabla}w\rangle\quad\text{ for every }u,w\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}.

Assume indeed that (
4.22
) holds true. Then, if
u\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
is fixed, (
4.22
) gives, by symmetry

(L_{c}u,w)_{H}=(u,L_{c}w)_{H}\quad\text{ for every }w\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty},

which shows that
u\in D(L_{c}^{*})
and
L_{c}^{*}u=L_{c}u
; since, as discussed at the end of Subsection
2.2.2
,
\boldsymbol{\Delta}=L_{c}^{*}
, this gives that
u\in D(\boldsymbol{\Delta})
and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}u=L_{c}u
. By the integration by parts formula in (
2.24
), we
deduce from (
4.22
) again (still assuming that it holds true) that

(4.23)

\langle{\mathrm{D}}u,{\mathrm{D}}w\rangle=\langle\boldsymbol{\nabla}u,\boldsymbol{\nabla}w\rangle\quad\text{ for every }w\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}.

Taking
w=u
, we see that
\|{\mathrm{D}}u\|=\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}u\|
; taking
w=w_{n}
for a sequence
(w_{n})_{n}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
such that
\boldsymbol{\nabla}w_{n}\to{\mathrm{D}}u
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\overline{{\mathcal{D}}};\mathbb{R}^{d})
as
n\to\infty
(see Proposition
2.13
and its proof) and passing to the limit as
n\to\infty
, we obtain

\langle\boldsymbol{\nabla}u,{\mathrm{D}}u\rangle=\langle{\mathrm{D}}u,{\mathrm{D}}u\rangle=\|{\mathrm{D}}u\|^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{\nabla}u\|\|{\mathrm{D}}u\|,

which gives
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u={\mathrm{D}}u
,
and completes the proof (provided (
4.22
) holds true).

Therefore, we are only left to show (
4.22
); let
u,w\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
and let, for every
\mathbf{s}\in T_{o}^{\infty}
,
u^{\mathbf{s}},w^{\mathbf{s}}\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
be the functions as in Definition
4.13
, for
u
and
w
respectively.
We show that (
4.22
) holds true by following
[
26
, Theorem 5.11]
. We have

\displaystyle(L_{c}u,w)_{H}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(L_{c}u)_{\mu}w(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}(L_{c}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)})_{\mu}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{\Delta_{z}\rvert_{z=x}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}(\mu+\mu_{x}\delta_{z}-\mu_{x}\delta_{x})}{(\mu_{x})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{\Delta_{z}\rvert_{z=x}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu_{r}^{x})}(\mu_{r}^{x}+r\delta_{z}-r\delta_{x})}{r^{2}}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu_{r}^{x})}(\mu_{r}^{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{d}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{\Delta_{z}\rvert_{z=x}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)}(\mu_{r}^{x}+r\delta_{z}-r\delta_{x})}{r^{2}}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)}(\mu_{r}^{x})\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{d}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{\nabla_{z}\rvert_{z=x}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)}(\mu_{r}^{x}+r\delta_{z}-r\delta_{x})}{r}\cdot\frac{\nabla_{z}\rvert_{z=x}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)}(\mu_{r}^{x}+r\delta_{z}-r\delta_{x})}{r}\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{d}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)})_{\mu_{r}^{x}}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{\nabla}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)})_{\mu_{r}^{x}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{d}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu_{r}^{x})})_{\mu_{r}^{x}}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{\nabla}w^{\mathbf{s}(\mu_{r}^{x})})_{\mu_{r}^{x}}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathrm{vol}_{d}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)})_{\mu}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)})_{\mu}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=-\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u)_{\mu}(x)\cdot(\boldsymbol{\nabla}w)_{\mu}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=-\langle\boldsymbol{\nabla}u,\boldsymbol{\nabla}w\rangle,

where we have used, in order, the definition of
L_{c}u
(on the second line), the definition of
L_{c}u^{\mathbf{s}}
(on the third line), the Mecke identity (
2.7
) (to pass from the third to the fourth line), the fact that the set
\{(x,\mu):\mu_{x}>0\}
is
\mathrm{vol}_{d}\otimes\mathcal{D}
-negligible (see
[
26
, Proposition 4.9(iii)]
so that
\mathbf{s}(\mu_{x}^{r})
does not depend on
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
and therefore we can denote it by
\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)
(to get the fifth line), integration by parts on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
(on the sixth line), the relation (
2.12
) (on the seventh line), again that
\mathbf{s}(\mu_{r}^{x})=\mathbf{s}(\mu,r)
(on the eight line), one more time the Mecke identity (
2.7
) (to obtain the ninth line), and, finally, the definition of
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
and
\boldsymbol{\nabla}w
(to get the tenth line). This shows (
4.22
) and concludes the proof.
∎

Proposition 4.15
.

For a given
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let
f\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}
and
g\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}
. Then, if
u
is the unique solution of

\partial_{t}u_{t}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{t}=f\quad\text{for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad u_{t}\bigr|_{t=T}=g,

there exist Borel representatives of
u
,
{\mathrm{D}}u
, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}u

satisfying the following properties: for every
\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
, there exists
v^{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\infty}([0,T])
such that

(4.24)

\displaystyle u_{t}(\mu)

\displaystyle=v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T],

(4.25)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}(\mu,\cdot)

\displaystyle={\mathrm{D}}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(\mu,\cdot),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T],

(4.26)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{t}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Delta}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T].

In particular
u_{t}\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
for every
t\in[0,T].

Proof.

Recall that the map
\mathrm{em}
as in (
2.4
) is injective on
T^{\infty}_{o}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o}
and we write its Borel measurable inverse
\mathrm{em}^{-1}:\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to T^{\infty}_{o}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o}
in the form

(\mathbf{s}(\mu),\mathbf{x}(\mu)):=\mathrm{em}^{-1}(\mu),\quad\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

For every
\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
, we set
n(\mathbf{s}):=N(\varepsilon,\mathbf{s})
, the latter being defined in (
2.32
); intuitively,

n(\mathbf{s})
is the largest index below which masses contained in the sequence
\mathbf{s}
are greater than or equal to
\varepsilon
. Note that
\mathbf{s}\mapsto n(\mathbf{s})
is a Borel map.

Construction of
v^{\mathbf{s}}
: let
(s_{j})_{j}=\mathbf{s}\in T_{o}^{\infty}
be fixed. When
n(\mathbf{s})=0
, we set
v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}:=0
, for all

t\in[0,T]
.
When

n(\mathbf{s})>0
, the construction of
v^{\mathbf{s}}
is more delicate and proceeds in several steps. We first set

\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{s}):=\frac{1}{2}\left(\min\{|s_{i}-s_{j}|:i,j\leq n(\mathbf{s}),\,i\neq j\}\wedge\min\{s_{j}-\varepsilon/2:j\leq n(\mathbf{s})\}\right),

which is related both to the minimal distance between the masses greater than or equal to
\varepsilon
, and to the minimal distance from these masses to
\varepsilon/2
.
Given
i\in\{1,\dots,dn(\mathbf{s})\}
, we call
(k,j)
the unique pair of integers
k\in\{0,\dots,n(\mathbf{s})-1\}
and
j\in\{1,\dots,d\}
such that
i=kd+j
, and then, we find a function
\hat{p}_{i}^{\mathbf{s}}\in{\mathrm{C}}^{\infty}([0,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d})
with
\mathop{\rm supp}\nolimits(\hat{p}_{i}^{\mathbf{s}})\subset[\varepsilon/2,1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
satisfying, for any
(r,x)\in[\varepsilon/2,1]\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

\hat{p}_{i}^{\mathbf{s}}(r,x^{1},\dots,x^{d})=\begin{cases}x^{j}/r\quad&\text{if }\ (r,x)\in[s_{k+1}-\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{s})/2,(s_{k+1}+\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{s})/2)\wedge 1]\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\\
0\quad&\text{if }\ (r,x)\in\left([0,s_{k+1}-\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{s})]\cup[(s_{k+1}+\mathrm{sep}(\mathbf{s}))\wedge 1,1]\right)\times\mathbb{T}^{d},\end{cases}

where
x^{j}
denotes the
j
-th coordinate of
x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}
. We set
\hat{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathbf{s}}:=(\hat{p}_{1}^{\mathbf{s}},\dots,\hat{p}_{dn(\mathbf{s})}^{\mathbf{s}})
.
It is clear from this definition, and with the notation
(
2.8
),
that,

(\hat{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathbf{s}})^{\star}(\mu)=(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})\in(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}\quad\text{ for every }\mu=\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x}),\quad(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})\in T^{\infty}_{o}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o}.

We define two smooth functions
f^{\mathbf{s}},g^{\mathbf{s}}:(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}\to\mathbb{R}
by letting

(4.27)

f^{\mathbf{s}}:=(f\circ\mathrm{em}^{n(\mathbf{s})})(\mathbf{s},\cdot),\quad g^{\mathbf{s}}:=(g\circ\mathrm{em}^{n(\mathbf{s})})(\mathbf{s},\cdot),

where
\mathrm{em}^{n}
is as in (
2.31
). We then consider, on
[0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}
, the PDE

(4.28)

\partial_{t}h_{t}+\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h_{t}=f^{\mathbf{s}}\quad\text{ for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad h_{t}\bigr|_{t=T}=g^{\mathbf{s}},

where
\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}
is the operator given by

\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h:=\sum_{j=1}^{n(\mathbf{s})}\frac{1}{s_{i}}(\Delta_{i}h),\quad h\in{\mathrm{C}}^{2}((\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}),

with
\Delta_{i}
being the Laplacian operator on
\mathbb{T}^{d}
, acting on the
i
-th (
d
-dimensional) component of
(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}
. The existence of a smooth solution
h^{\mathbf{s}}:[0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}\to\mathbb{R}
to (
4.28
) follows from the fact that it can be expressed as

h^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}(x):=k^{\mathbf{s}}_{T-t}(A_{\mathbf{s}}^{-1/2}x),\quad\text{ for every }(t,x)\in[0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})},

where
A_{\mathbf{s}}
is the diagonal matrix
of size
n(\mathbf{s})\times n(\mathbf{s})

with
(A_{\mathbf{s}})_{jj}=s_{i}^{-1}
for
j\in\{kd+1,\dots,(k+1)d\}
,
k\in\{0,\dots,n(\mathbf{s})-1\}
, and
k^{\mathbf{s}}:[0,T]\times(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}\to\mathbb{R}
is the smooth solution of the heat equation

(4.29)

\partial_{t}k_{t}-\Delta k_{t}=-\tilde{f}^{\mathbf{s}}(A_{\mathbf{s}}^{1/2}(\cdot))\quad\text{ for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad k_{t}\bigr|_{t=0}=\tilde{g}^{\mathbf{s}}(A_{\mathbf{s}}^{1/2}(\cdot)),

with
\tilde{f}^{\mathbf{s}}
and
\tilde{g}^{\mathbf{s}}

being the periodic extensions of the smooth functions
f^{\mathbf{s}}
and
g^{\mathbf{s}}
to
(\mathbb{R}^{d})^{n(\mathbf{s})}
; see e.g.
[
42
, Exercise 9.1.3]
for an existence result to the PDE. Classical estimates on the solution to the heat equation (
4.29
) (see e.g.
[
42
, Exercise 9.1.4]
) and the fact that
s_{i}\geq\varepsilon
for every
i\in\{1,\dots,n(\mathbf{s})\}
with
n(\mathbf{s})\leq\varepsilon^{-1}
, give the existence of a constant
C
, depending on
\varepsilon
,
f
, and
g
but not on
\mathbf{s}
, such that

(4.30)

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{n(\mathbf{s})}\frac{1}{s_{i}}\|\nabla_{i}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}\|_{\infty}+\|\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}\|_{\infty}+\|h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}\|_{\infty}\right)\leq C.

When
n(\mathbf{s})>0
, we set
v^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}:=h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}\circ(\hat{\mathbf{p}}^{\mathbf{s}})^{\star}
,
t\in[0,T]
. It is clear from the construction that
v^{\mathbf{s}}\in\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon/2}^{\infty}([0,T])
, in fact for every
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
the function
t\mapsto v^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}(\mu)
belongs to
{\mathrm{C}}^{\infty}([0,T])
, being
h^{\mathbf{s}}
smooth.

Construction of
u
, its derivatives, and their measurability
:
following item (1) in Definition
4.13
, we define
the function
u:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
as

(4.31)

u_{t}(\mu):=v^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}_{t}(\mu),\quad(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Let us also set

(4.32)

(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u_{t})_{\mu}:=(\boldsymbol{\nabla}v^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}_{t})_{\mu},\quad(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}:=(L_{c}v^{\mathbf{s}(\mu)}_{t})_{\mu}\quad\text{ for every }(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

We now show simpler representations of the above operators: for every
t\in[0,T]
we have

(4.33)

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u_{t})_{\mu}(x_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{s_{i}}\nabla_{i}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for every }\mu=\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x}),\,(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})\in T_{o}^{\infty}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{o}^{\infty},\,i=1,\dots,n(\mathbf{s}),

(4.34)

\displaystyle(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for every }\mu=\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x}),\,(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})\in T_{o}^{\infty}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{o}^{\infty},

where, in both formulas, we mean that, if
n(\mathbf{s})=0
, then both right-hand sides are equal to the constant function
0
. The proof of the above formulas relies on the following two arguments: if
n(\mathbf{s})=0
, then
v^{\mathbf{s}}=0
and the formulas follow; if
n(\mathbf{s})>0
, (
4.33
) is a simple consequence of the definition of
v^{\mathbf{s}}
and Lemma
2.8
, while (
4.34
) can be obtained by simply computing

\displaystyle(L_{c}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}})_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\frac{\Delta_{z}\rvert_{z=x}v^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}(\mu+\mu_{x}\delta_{z}-\mu_{x}\delta_{x})}{(\mu_{x})^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{s_{j}}\Delta_{z}\rvert_{z=x_{j}}v^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}\left(\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}s_{k}\delta_{x_{k}}+s_{j}\delta_{z}-s_{j}\delta_{x_{j}}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n(\mathbf{s})}\frac{1}{s_{j}}\Delta_{j}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})=\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h_{t}^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})}),

where we have also used that
\mathbf{s}=\mathbf{s}(\mu)=\mathbf{s}(\mu+\mu_{x}\delta_{z}-\mu_{x}\delta_{x})
. To show that
u
,
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
and
L_{c}u
are measurable functions,
we consider, for every
k\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, the
set

T^{k,\varepsilon}_{o}:=\pi_{k}\left(\{\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}:n(\mathbf{s})=k\}\right),

where
\pi_{k}
is the projection from
T_{o}^{\infty}
to
[0,1]^{k}
defined by
\pi_{k}(\mathbf{s})=(s_{1},\dots,s_{k})

and, for every
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})\in T_{o}^{k,\varepsilon}
,
h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}
is the smooth solution (see the discussion above for existence and uniqueness of it) of the PDE, on
[0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k}
,

(4.35)

\partial_{t}h_{t}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{r_{j}}\Delta_{j}h_{t}=f^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}\quad\text{ for a.e. }t\in(0,T),\quad h_{t}|_{t=T}=g^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}},

where
f^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}},g^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}:(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k}\to\mathbb{R}
are defined as

f^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}:=f^{\mathbf{s}},\quad g^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}:=g^{\mathbf{s}}

for any
\mathbf{s}\in\pi_{k}^{-1}(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
, observing that
f^{\mathbf{s}}
and
f^{\mathbf{s}^{\prime}}
are equal for any two
\mathbf{s},\mathbf{s}^{\prime}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
such that
n(\mathbf{s})=n(\mathbf{s}^{\prime})=k
and
\pi_{k}(\mathbf{s})=\pi_{k}(\mathbf{s}^{\prime})
. In particular, the PDE (
4.35
) can be regarded as a parametrized parabolic PDE whose coefficients depend in a continuous way on the parameter
(r_{1},\cdots,r_{k})\in T^{k,\varepsilon}_{o}
, with the latter space being (obviously) equipped with the induced topology. By standard Schauder theory, solutions to the PDE, regarded as real-valued functions defined on
[0,T]\times({\mathbb{T}}^{d})^{k}
, are once differentiable in time and twice in space. Together with their derivatives (of order one in time and of order two in space), they are jointly Hölder continuous, uniformly in
(r_{1},\cdots,r_{k})
. As a result, the mapping
that sends
(r_{1},\dots,r_{k})
onto
(h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}},(\partial_{i}h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}})_{i=1,\dots,dk},(\partial^{2}_{ij}h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}})_{i,j=1\dots,dk})
is continuous, when all the functions on the right-hand side are regarded as elements of
{\mathrm{C}}([0,T],(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k})

equipped with the
L^{\infty}
-norm.
Therefore, the map
F_{k}:T_{o}^{k,\varepsilon}\to{\mathrm{C}}([0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k})\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k})\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T]\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{k};\mathbb{R}^{d})^{k}
, defined by

F_{k}(r_{1},\dots,r_{k}):=\left(h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}},\sum_{j=1}^{k}\frac{1}{r_{j}}\Delta_{j}h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}},\left(\frac{1}{r_{j}}\nabla_{j}h^{r_{1},\dots,r_{k}}\right)_{j=1,\dots,k}\right)

is continuous when
the arrival space is equipped with the uniform convergence topology.
Denoting the components of
F_{k}
by
F_{k}^{i}
,
i=1,2,3
, and using (
4.33
) and (
4.34
) together with the identity
h^{\mathbf{s}}=h^{\pi_{k}(\mathbf{s})}
when
n(\mathbf{s})=k
, it is not difficult to check that
u
,
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
, and
L_{c}

can be expressed in the following way:

\displaystyle u_{t}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\left\lceil 1/\varepsilon\right\rceil}\mathbf{1}_{\{k\}}(n(\mathbf{s}(\mu)))[F_{k}^{1}(s_{1}(\mu),\dots,s_{k}(\mu))](t,x_{1}(\mu),\dots,x_{k}(\mu)),

\displaystyle(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\left\lceil 1/\varepsilon\right\rceil}\mathbf{1}_{\{k\}}(n(\mathbf{s}(\mu)))[F_{k}^{2}(s_{1}(\mu),\dots,s_{k}(\mu))](t,x_{1}(\mu),\dots,x_{k}(\mu)),

\displaystyle(\boldsymbol{\nabla}u_{t})_{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{\left\lceil 1/\varepsilon\right\rceil}\mathbf{1}_{\{k\}}(n(\mathbf{s}(\mu)))\sum_{j=1}^{k}\{[F_{k}^{3}(s_{1}(\mu),\dots,s_{k}(\mu))](t,x_{1}(\mu),\dots,x_{k}(\mu))\}_{j}\mathbf{1}_{\{x_{j}(\mu)\}}(x),

for every
(t,\mu,x)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}
, where we have denoted with a subscript
j
the components of
F^{3}_{k}
. The above expressions, the aforementioned continuity properties of
F_{k}
, and the measurability of
\mathrm{em}^{-1}
, give the joint measurability of
u
,
\boldsymbol{\nabla}u
and
L_{c}u

with respect to their respective entries, i.e.,
t
,
\mu
and possibly
x
.
Finally, the definition of
u
and the measurability properties just established, together with the bounds in (
4.30
), give that, for every
t\in[0,T]
,
u_{t}\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{fw}^{\infty}
. Hence, by Proposition
4.14
, we get (
4.25
) and (
4.26
).

The equation solved by
u
: we show that
u
solves

(4.36)

\partial_{t}u_{t}(\mu)+(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}=f(\mu),\quad u_{T}(\mu)=g(\mu)\quad\text{ for every }(t,\mu)\in(0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d}).

Let
(t,\mu)\in(0,T)\times\mathcal{P}_{o}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
with
\mu=\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})
for some
(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})\in T_{o}^{\infty}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{o}^{\infty}
. If
n(\mathbf{s})=0
, then

\partial_{t}u_{t}(\mu)=\partial_{t}v^{\mathbf{s}}(\mu)=(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}=(L_{c}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s}})_{\mu}=0,

where we used the definition of
u
and (
4.34
) together with the fact that, in this case,
v^{\mathbf{s}}=0
; since, in this case, we also have
f(\mu)=g(\mu)=0
(as
f
and
g
belong to
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}
), we deduce that (
4.36
) is satisfied at
(t,\mu)
. If
n(\mathbf{s})>0
, we have

\partial_{t}u_{t}(\mu)=\partial_{t}v^{\mathbf{s}}_{t}(\mu)=\partial_{t}h^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})=-\Delta^{\mathbf{s}}h^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})+f^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})=-(L_{c}u_{t})_{\mu}+f(\mu),

where we have used the definition of
u
, (
4.34
), the PDE (
4.28
), the definition
of
f^{\mathbf{s}}
in
(
4.27
), and again the fact that
f\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}
. Using the same properties and the definition of
g^{\mathbf{s}}
(also in
(
4.27
)), it is easy to see that
u_{T}(\mu)=g(\mu)
. This shows that (
4.36
) is satisfied at
(t,\mu)
also in this case. Finally, (
4.36
), (
4.32
), and (
4.26
) show that
u
solves the PDE in the statement thus concluding the proof.
∎

Remark 4.16
.

It follows immediately from the proof, that, for a given fixed
\mu\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the functions
t\mapsto v^{\mathbf{s}}(\mu)
belong to
{\mathrm{C}}^{\infty}([0,T])
and not only to
\mathop{\rm Lip}\nolimits_{b}([0,T],{\sf d}_{eucl})
because of the smoothness of the mapping

t\mapsto h^{\mathbf{s}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n(\mathbf{s})})
for a fixed value of
\mathbf{s}\in T_{o}^{\infty}
.

Corollary 4.17
.

Let
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H)
,
g\in H
, and
u
be the unique solution of

\partial_{t}u_{t}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{t}=f_{t}\quad\text{for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad u_{t}\bigr|_{t=T}=g.

Then, there exist sequences
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\subset(0,1)
,
(g^{n})_{n}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
and
(f^{n})_{n}\subset L^{2}([0,T];H)
,
satisfying the following two properties:

i.

for any
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
,
given the
unique solution
u^{n}
to

\partial_{t}u^{n}_{t}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u^{n}_{t}=f^{n}_{t}\quad\text{for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad u^{n}_{t}\bigr|_{t=T}=g^{n},

there exist Borel representatives of
u^{n}
,
{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}u^{n}
and, for any
\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
,
there exists also a function
v^{\mathbf{s},n}\in\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\infty}([0,T])
such that, at any
pair
(t,\mu)\in[0,T]\times\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})
, it holds

(4.37)

u_{t}^{n}(\mu)=v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu),\quad{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}^{n}(\mu,\cdot)={\mathrm{D}}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu,\cdot),\quad\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{t}^{n}(\mu)=\boldsymbol{\Delta}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu);

ii.

g^{n}\to g
in
H
,
f^{n}\to f
in
L^{2}([0,T];H)
, and

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|u^{n}_{t}-u_{t}|^{2}_{H}+\int_{0}^{T}\|{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}^{n}-{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.

Proof.

By density of
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
in
L^{2}(\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\mathcal{D})
(see (
2.9
)) we can find sequences
(g^{n})_{n}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
and
(f^{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{S}(0,T;\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty})
such that
g^{n}\to g
in
H
and
f^{n}\to f
in
L^{2}([0,T];H)
as
n\to\infty
, where we have denoted by
\mathcal{S}(0,T;\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty})
the vector space of simple (in time) functions taking values in
\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
.
In words, the two inclusions

(g^{n})_{n}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty}
and
(f^{n})_{n}\subset\mathcal{S}(0,T;\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{c}^{\infty})
mean that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, there exist values
\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}\in(0,1)

and
K_{n}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
, a partition
0=t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}^{n}<\dots<t_{K_{n}-1}^{n}<t_{K_{n}}^{n}=T
, and functions
(f_{i}^{n})_{i=1}^{K_{n}}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}}^{\infty}
, such that
g^{n}\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon^{\prime}_{n}}^{\infty}
and

f^{n}(t)=\sum_{i=0}^{K_{n}-1}\chi_{[t_{i}^{n},t_{i+1}^{n})}(t)f_{i}^{n}\quad\text{ for every }t\in[0,T).

For a given
h\in\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon}^{\infty}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, let us denote by
S_{t}^{i,n}h
the unique solution of

\partial_{t}v_{t}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}v_{t}=f_{i}^{n}\quad\text{for a.e.\penalty\ }t\in(0,T),\quad v_{t}\bigr|_{t=T}=h.

We deduce from Proposition
4.15
that there exist Borel representatives of
S_{t}^{i,n}h
, its gradient, and its Laplacian, and,
for every
\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
,
there exists also

v^{\mathbf{s},i,n,h}\in\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\delta_{n}}^{\infty}([0,T])
,
with
\delta_{n}:=(\varepsilon_{n}\wedge\varepsilon)/2
,
such that

\displaystyle S_{t}^{i,n}h(\mu)

\displaystyle=v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},i,n,h}(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T],

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}(S_{t}^{i,n}h)(\mu,\cdot)

\displaystyle={\mathrm{D}}(v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},i,n,h})(\mu,\cdot),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T],

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}(S_{t}^{i,n}h)(\mu)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Delta}(v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},i,n,h})(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\text{ and }t\in[0,T].

Now define

u_{T}^{n}:=g^{n},\quad u^{n}_{t}:=\sum_{i=0}^{K_{n}-1}S^{i,n}_{T-t_{i+1}^{n}+t}u^{n}_{t_{i+1}^{n}}\chi_{[t_{i}^{n},t_{i+1}^{n}]}(t)\quad t\in[0,T].

Combining
the above construction with
Proposition
3.6
, we easily deduce that
the sequence

(u^{n})_{n}

satisfies
i and ii.
∎

Our goal is to relate (
3.10
) to the system of interacting particles (
4.1
) (when driven by a drift
b
that is independent of
i
) with the language of the canonical process as in Subsection
2.3.1
, see also Subsection
4.1.2
.

Proposition 4.18
.

Let
b:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded and measurable function,
t_{0}\in[0,T)
be an initial time,
p
be a bounded
{\mathcal{D}}
-density,
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H)
, and
g\in H
be a final datum. If
u
is the unique solution to (
3.3
), then the process
(u_{t}(\mu_{t}^{\infty}))_{t\in[t_{0},T]}
can be expanded
in the following two equivalent ways:

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}r-\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},x)\cdot b_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{\infty}_{r}(x)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\,\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.e.},

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\,\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.e.}\,,

where

\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(p{\mathcal{D}})}

is defined as in Proposition

4.3

(it being understood that
all the

b^{i}
’s in
(
4.1
)
are equal to
b
).

Note that in the above display we are using the convention explained in Remark
2.15
to evaluate
{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\cdot)
at
\xi_{r}^{i}
.

Proof.

Recalling the notation
(
3.1
),
let us set
t\in[0,T]\mapsto k_{t}:=f_{t}-\left[b,{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}\right]\in H
.
Since
b
is bounded and
u\in L^{2}([0,T];H^{1,2})
,
k
belongs to
L^{2}([0,T];H)
, which makes it possible to apply Corollary
4.17
. We then introduce
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n}\subset(0,1)
,
(g^{n})_{n}\subset\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}^{\infty}_{c}
,
(k^{n})_{n}\subset L^{2}([0,T];H)
,
(u^{n})_{n}\subset L^{2}([0,T];H^{1,2})

(v^{\mathbf{s},n})_{n}\in\prod_{n=1}^{\infty}\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\infty}([0,T])
, the sequences provided by Corollary
4.17
(note
f_{t}
there is
k_{t}
here). Thanks to the latter, there exist Borel representatives of
u^{n}
,
{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}
, and
\boldsymbol{\Delta}u^{n}
such that, for every
\mathbf{s}\in T^{\infty}_{o}
,

(4.38)

\displaystyle u_{t}^{n}(\mu)

\displaystyle=v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\ \text{and}\ t\in[0,T],

(4.39)

\displaystyle{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}^{n}(\mu,\cdot)

\displaystyle={\mathrm{D}}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu,\cdot),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\ \text{and}\ t\in[0,T],

(4.40)

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{t}^{n}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Delta}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu),\quad

\displaystyle\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\ \text{and}\ t\in[0,T].

Note that (
4.38
) also gives that

(4.41)

\partial_{t}u_{t}^{n}(\mu)=\partial_{t}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu)\quad\text{ for all }\mu\in\mathrm{em}(\mathbf{s},(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o})\ \text{and a.e.\penalty\ }t\in[0,T],

where both functions are regarded as representatives of Sobolev derivatives, see also the proof of Proposition
2.18
.
For fixed
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})\in T_{o}^{\infty}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})_{o}^{\infty}
, since
v^{\mathbf{s},n}\in\mathfrak{T}\hat{\mathfrak{Z}}_{\varepsilon_{n}}^{\infty}([0,T])
, we can apply Proposition
2.18
and obtain that

(4.42)

\begin{split}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})&=v_{t_{0}}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\partial_{r}v_{t}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}v_{r}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\right)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}v_{r}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})}\text{-a.s.},\end{split}

where we recall Remark
2.15
for the meaning of the evaluation of
{\mathrm{D}}v_{r}^{\mathbf{s},n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\cdot)
at
\xi_{r}^{i}
.
Using the above relations (
4.38
), (
4.39
), (
4.40
), and (
4.41
), we can write

(4.43)

\begin{split}u_{t}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})&=u_{t_{0}}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})+\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\partial_{r}u_{r}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{r}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\right)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})}\text{-a.s.}\end{split}

Observe that, in the above formula, the process defined by the stochastic integral depends in principle on
n
and
(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})
. However, by Lemma
4.7
and Corollary
4.8
applied to
b=2{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}
, we can assume that it only depends on
n
, provided that
(\mathbf{s},\mathbf{x})=\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)
for
m
varying in a full
{\mathcal{D}}
-measure subset of
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
. In other words, for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
there exists a progressively-measurable mapping
\mathcal{I}^{t_{0},n}:\Xi\to{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that for
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e.
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and for every bounded
{\mathcal{D}}
-density
q
it holds

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\left(\left\{\forall t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\mathcal{I}_{t}^{t_{0},n}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\right\}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(q{\mathcal{D}})}\left(\left\{\forall t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\mathcal{I}_{t}^{t_{0},n}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\right\}\right)=1.

Therefore, we can assume that for
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e.
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the equality in (
4.43
) is a
\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
-a.e. equality between Borel functions in
\Xi
which only depends on
n
. By the disintegration formula (
4.15
) for
\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}
and (
4.43
), we get that for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
it holds

(4.44)

\begin{split}u_{t}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})-u_{t_{0}}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})&=\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\left(\partial_{r}u_{r}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{r}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\right)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\quad\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.s.}\end{split}

Using the additive structure of the right-hand side, we can easily write a similar formula but for the increment

g^{n}(\mu_{T}^{\infty})-u_{t}(\mu_{t}^{\infty})
(in place of

u_{t}(\mu_{t}^{\infty})-u_{t_{0}}(\mu_{t_{0}}^{\infty})
). And then, using the fact that
\partial_{r}u_{r}^{n}+\boldsymbol{\Delta}u_{r}^{n}=k_{r}^{n}
(see Corollary
4.17
) together with the fact that
(\mu_{t}^{\infty})_{\sharp}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\ll{\mathcal{D}}
for every
t\in[t_{0},T]
by (
4.16
), we obtain for every
n\in\mathbb{N}_{+}

(4.45)

g^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{T})=u_{t}^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}k_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t}^{T}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\,\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.s.}

Now we show that every term in the above equality converges as
n\to\infty
to the corresponding term without the apex
n
. Using that
(\mu_{t}^{\infty})_{\sharp}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\leq\|p\|_{\infty}{\mathcal{D}}
(again, coming from (
4.16
)), we get

\displaystyle\int_{\Xi}\lvert g^{n}(\mu^{\infty}_{T})-g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\lvert g^{n}(\mu)-g(\mu)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}[(\mu^{\infty}_{T})_{\sharp}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}](\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\|p\|_{\infty}|g^{n}-g|_{H}^{2}\to 0,\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

the convergence of the last term to
0
following from the properties stated in Corollary
4.17
. Similarly,
using again the conclusion of Corollary
4.17
, one can show that

\displaystyle\int_{\Xi}\lvert u^{n}_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})-u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})\rvert^{2}]\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\to 0,\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

\displaystyle\int_{\Xi}\left\lvert\int_{t}^{T}(k^{n}_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})-k_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r}))\,\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}

\displaystyle\hskip 30.0pt\leq T\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\Xi}\lvert k_{r}^{n}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})-k_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\,\mathrm{d}r\to 0,\quad\text{as }n\to\infty.

Finally, let us focus on the stochastic integral. By Itô isometry and thanks, once again, to (
4.16
), it holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Xi}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t}^{T}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}\left({\mathrm{D}}u^{n}_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})-{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\right)\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}\,\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\int_{\Xi}\int_{t}^{T}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\varsigma_{i}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})-{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi^{i}_{r})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}

\displaystyle\qquad=2\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\Xi}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},x)-{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{\infty}_{r}(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad\leq 2\|p\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}_{r}(\mu,x)-{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu,x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\,\mathrm{d}\mathcal{D(\mu)}\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\qquad=2\|p\|_{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\|{\mathrm{D}}u^{n}_{r}-{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}\|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\to 0,\quad\text{as }n\to\infty,

where the last line follows from Corollary
4.17
. Thus, we can let
n
tend to
\infty
in
(
4.45
). In the limit, we obtain the same formula, but without the apex
n
, as we announced earlier.
Recalling that
k_{t}=f_{t}-\left[b,{\mathrm{D}}u_{t}\right]
, we obtain

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}r-\int_{t}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},x)\cdot b(\mu^{\infty}_{r},x)\,\mathrm{d}\mu^{\infty}_{r}(x)\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\,\mathbf{P}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.s.}

By Girsanov theorem, the sum of the last two terms on the right-hand side can be written as a stochastic integral, driven by a new Brownian motion under the probability
(z_{T}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})})^{-1}{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}
, see
(
4.10
).
Using the transformation

\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{b}
,
see (
4.8
) (with the bold superscript
{\boldsymbol{b}}
being replaced by
b
since the
b^{i}
’s are all equal to
b
), the resulting expansion can be transferred onto the canonical space.
We obtain

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\,\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}(p{\mathcal{D}})}\text{-a.s.,}

which completes the proof.
∎

The following result, which constitutes the main finding of this section,
provides a Kolmogorov-type representation formula for the solution of the
transport-diffusion equation (
3.10
) in terms of the drifted particle
system (
4.1
).

Theorem 4.19
.

Let
b:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a bounded and measurable function, and
g\in H
be a final datum. If
u
is the unique solution to (
3.10
), then, for every
t_{0}\in[0,T)
, there exists a Borel subset
O
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{D}}(O)=1
, such that

(4.46)

u_{t_{0}}(m)=\mathbb{E}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}[g(\mu_{T}^{\infty})],\quad\text{ for every }m\in O,

where
{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}
is the expectation under
{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
.

Remark 4.20
.

The set
O
appearing in the statement of Theorem
4.19
depends on the choice of
t_{0}\in[0,T)
.
This dependence will also occur in several statements throughout the remainder of the paper.
Rather than making it explicit each time, as in Theorem
4.19
, we shall simply write that the equality holds “for every
t_{0}\in[0,T)
and
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e.
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
”.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two steps: the first establishes a weaker version of a (
4.46
), which is then combined with (
4.18
) in the second step to obtain the desired representation formula.

First step:

The objective of this step is to prove that

u_{t_{0}}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}({\mathcal{D}})}}[g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\rvert\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}}],\quad\text{for every $t_{0}\in[0,T)$ and ${\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}({\mathcal{D}})}$\penalty\ -a.e.}

Let us denote
\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}:=\mathrm{em}_{\sharp}^{-1}({\mathcal{D}})
; by Proposition
4.18
with
f=0
,
p=1
, and
t=t_{0}

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t_{0}}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t_{0}}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}\text{-a.e.}

If we assume that the stochastic integral is a
\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}
-martingale, then (
4.46
) follows by taking the conditional expectation with respect to
\mu_{t_{0}}^{\infty}
.

To prove the martingale property of the stochastic integral, we argue as follows.
Since the stochastic integral is already known to be a local martingale under
\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}
, it suffices to show that

(4.47)

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}\right\rvert\right]<\infty.

In order to prove (
4.47
), let us recall the following two estimates. First, by Proposition
2.14
and Theorem
3.4
, we have

(4.48)

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\varsigma_{i}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]\leq\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu,x)\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\overline{\mathcal{D}}(\mu,x)\,\mathrm{d}r<\infty.

Second, recalling the definition of
z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}
in (
4.10
), we have

(4.49)

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[(z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}})^{-2}\right]\leq 2\exp\{T\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}^{2}\}.

And then, by the definition of
\mathbf{Q}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}
in (
4.8
), we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}\right\rvert\right]

(4.50)

\displaystyle\quad\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\frac{1}{z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}\right\rvert\right]

(4.51)

\displaystyle\qquad+{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\frac{1}{z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\varsigma_{i}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot b_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert\right].

By combining Cauchy-Schwarz inequality with Doob’s inequality and the two bounds (
4.48
) and (
4.49
), we obtain

(
4.50
)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[(z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}})^{-2}\right]^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}\right\rvert^{2}\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq 4e^{\frac{1}{2}T\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}^{2}}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\varsigma_{i}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

Similarly,

(
4.51
)

\displaystyle\leq{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[(z_{T}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}})^{-2}\right]^{1/2}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sup_{t\in[t_{0},T]}\left\lvert\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{t}_{t_{0}}\varsigma_{i}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot b(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r\right\rvert^{2}\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}e^{\frac{1}{2}T\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}^{2}}\sqrt{T}\left\|b\right\|_{\infty}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\textrm{\rm\text{{m}}}}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t_{0}}^{T}\varsigma_{i}\lvert{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\rvert^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

Gathering the last three displays, we get (
4.47
), which
concludes the proof of the first step.

Second step:
From the first step, it follows that for any bounded and measurable
p\colon\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}({\mathcal{D}})}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})u_{t_{0}}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})\right]={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}({\mathcal{D}})}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})g(\mu_{T}^{\infty})\right].

Then, by (
4.18
), we can rewrite the latter as

(4.52)

\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})u_{t_{0}}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})g(\mu_{T}^{\infty})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m).

On the one hand, the left-hand side of (
4.52
) is equal to

\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})u_{t_{0}}(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}p(m)u_{t_{0}}(m)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m),

since
\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}}=m
holds
{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
-a.e..
On the other hand, the right-hand side of (
4.52
) is equal to

\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[p(\mu^{\infty}_{t_{0}})g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}p(m){\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m).

Then, by combining the last two displays, we deduce that

\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}p(m){\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\right]\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m)=\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}p(m)u_{t_{0}}(m)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m),

and, finally, we deduce that, for
{\mathcal{D}}
-a.e.
m
,

u_{t_{0}}(m)={\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})\right].

∎

We conclude this section with a different version of Proposition
4.18
which will be needed when addressing the control problem in Section
5
. The main difference concerns the fact that the subset of
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
of full
{\mathcal{D}}
-measure at which the Itô expansion holds true, is independent of the drift. In the statement below, the latter is denoted by
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=(\alpha^{i})_{i}
and is thus allowed to depend on
i
, as originally considered in the beginning of this section. Accordingly, we denote by

{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
the probability measure introduced in Proposition
4.3
,
when
(b^{i})_{i}
is given by

(\alpha^{i})_{i}
.

Proposition 4.21
.

Let
t_{0}\in[0,T)
be a initial time,
f\in L^{2}([0,T];H)
and
g\in H
. If
u
is the unique solution of (
3.3
) with
b=0
, then there exists a Borel subset
O
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, with
{\mathcal{D}}(O)=1
, such that,
for any
m\in O
and any collection of uniformly bounded and measurable functions

(\alpha^{i}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d})_{i}
, it holds

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{t}^{T}\varsigma_{i}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi_{r}^{i})\cdot\alpha_{r}^{i}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi_{r}^{i})\ \,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r},\quad t\in[t_{0},T],\quad{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\alpha}\text{-a.s.}

Note that in the above display we are using the convention explained in Remark
2.15
to evaluate
{\mathrm{D}}u_{r}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\cdot)
and
\alpha_{r}^{i}(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\cdot)
at
\xi_{r}^{i}
.

Proof.

The proof is divided into two steps.

First step:
Handling the case
\alpha^{i}=0
, for all
i\in{\mathbb{N}}_{+}
, and defining the set
O
.

By Proposition
4.18
with
b=0
and
p=1
, we know that, with probability 1 under
{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}({\mathcal{D}})}
, for all
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma^{i}}{{\mathrm{D}}}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}.

Note that, the result above holds for one arbitrary version of
{{\mathrm{D}}}u
.
Then, by the second display in the proof of Lemma
4.7
, with
b=2{{\mathrm{D}}}u
(at least,
for one version of the latter), we deduce that there exists a progressively measurable mapping

{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}:\Xi\rightarrow{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
such that
{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{T}-{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}
coincides
{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D})}
-a.s. with the stochastic integral appearing in the expansion right above. The statement of Lemma
4.7
says that

{\mathcal{I}}_{T}^{t_{0}}-{\mathcal{I}}_{t}^{t_{0}}
also coincides with the same stochastic integral, but

{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
-a.s., for
m
belonging to a Borel subset
O_{1}
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
such that

{\mathcal{D}}(O_{1})=1
.
We thus rewrite the above identity in the form

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D})}\left(\left\{\forall t\in[0,T],\quad g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{T}-{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}\right\}\right)=1.

Using the disintegration formula (
4.13
), we can write this as

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\left(\left\{\forall t\in[0,T],\quad g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{T}-{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}\right\}\right)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(m)=1,

which implies that there exists a Borel subset
O_{2}
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
such that

{\mathcal{D}}(O_{2})=1
and, for any
m\in O_{2}
,

\displaystyle{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\left(\left\{\forall t\in[0,T],\quad g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{T}-{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}\right\}\right)=1.

On
O_{1}\cap O_{2}
(which has again measure equal to 1 under
{\mathcal{D}}
),
the following two expansions hold true

{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
-a.s.,
for any
t\in[t_{0},T]
,

(4.53)

\displaystyle g(\mu^{\infty}_{T})

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{T}-{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}

\displaystyle=u_{t}(\mu^{\infty}_{t})+\int_{t}^{T}f_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r})\,\mathrm{d}r+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int^{T}_{t}\sqrt{2\varsigma_{i}}{\rm D}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi^{i}_{r})\cdot\,\mathrm{d}\beta^{i}_{r}.

Finally, we recall that, by Proposition
2.14
,

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}_{\sharp}(\mathcal{D})}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\varsigma^{i}\left|{\rm D}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi_{r}^{i})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]=\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{d}}|{\rm D}u_{r}(\mu,x)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}\mu(x)\right)\,\mathrm{d}{\mathcal{D}}(\mu)\,\mathrm{d}r<\infty,

which implies, again thanks to (
4.13
), that there exists a Borel subset
O_{3}
of
{\mathcal{P}}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, with

{\mathcal{D}}(O_{3})=1
, such that, for any
m\in O_{3}
,

(4.54)

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{T}\varsigma^{i}\left|{\rm D}u_{r}(\mu^{\infty}_{r},\xi_{r}^{i})\right|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}r\right]<\infty.

For the remaining part of the proof, we set
O:=O_{1}\cap O_{2}\cap O_{3}
, and we note that
{\mathcal{D}}(O)=1
.

Second step:

Handling the general case.

The purpose of this step is to transfer
(
4.53
)
from the canonical space equipped with

{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}

to the canonical space equipped with

{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
,
for a given collection
of uniformly
bounded and measurable functions
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}=(\alpha^{i}:[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\times\mathbb{T}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d})_{i}
, and for any
m\in O
. This step is divided in several sub-steps.

Sub-step 2.a:
Revisiting some definitions and results from Subsection
4.1
.

We first recall formula (
4.8
) (with
\boldsymbol{b}
therein being now understood
as
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
), which holds true for any initial measure

m
on
T^{\infty}_{o}\times(\mathbb{T}^{d})^{\infty}_{o}
and in particular under

\delta_{\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}
, for
m\in O
:

(4.55)

{\mathbf{Q}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\boldsymbol{\alpha}}={\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{\sharp}\left(\frac{1}{z_{T}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\right).

Note that we emphasized the dependence of
z_{T}
on both the initial measure
\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)
and the collection of velocity fields
{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
, as well as the dependence
of
\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
on
\boldsymbol{\alpha}
.
We also recall (as a consequence of Girsanov theorem) that

(4.56)

{\mathbb{E}}^{{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}}\left[\frac{1}{z_{T}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}\right]=1.

The second point we wish to emphasize is that, from the proof of
Lemma
4.7
, we can construct a sequence of Riemann sums
({\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0},(n)})_{n}
such that, for any

m\in O
,

\forall\varepsilon>0,\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\left(\left\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}-{\mathcal{I}}_{t}^{t_{0},(n)}|\geq\varepsilon\right\}\right)=0.

By combining the last two displays, we deduce that, for the same fixed collection

{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
as before,

(4.57)

\forall\varepsilon>0,\quad\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left[\frac{1}{z_{T}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m),\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}{\mathbf{P}}^{t_{0},\mathrm{em}^{-1}(m)}\right]\left(\left\{\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|{\mathcal{I}}^{t_{0}}_{t}-{\mathcal{I}}_{t}^{t_{0},(n)}|\geq\varepsilon\right\}\right)=0.

For the purpose of the proof, we introduce,
for each

,

\begin{split}\theta_{i}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}:T^{\infty}_{o}\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\infty}\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})&\rightarrow{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})\\
\left((s_{j})_{j},(x^{j})_{j},w\right)&\mapsto\left(w_{t}+\sqrt{\frac{s_{i}}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\alpha_{r}^{i}\left(\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}s_{j}\delta_{x^{j}_{r}},x_{r}^{i}\right)\,\mathrm{d}r\right)_{t\in[0,T]},\end{split}

where we are using the convention as in Remark
2.15
to evaluate
\alpha_{r}^{i}
at
x_{r}^{i}
, see also (

) for the meaning of

. We define the mapping
{\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\colon\Xi\to\Xi
as

{\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope {{{}}}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}:\left((s_{j})_{j},(x_{j}^{0})_{j},(w^{j})_{j},(x^{j})_{j}\right)\mapsto\left((s_{j})_{j},(x_{j}^{0}),\left(\theta_{j}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}((s_{i})_{i},(x^{i})_{i},w^{j})\right)_{j},(x^{j})_{j}\right).

Recalling the definition of
\psi_{i}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}
given in (

), we observe that

\theta_{i}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left((s_{j})_{j},(x^{j})_{j},\psi_{i}^{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\left((s_{j})_{j},(x^{j})_{j},w\right)\right)=(w_{t})_{t\in[0,T]}

for every

and every
\left((s_{j})_{j},(x_{j}^{0})_{j},(w^{j})_{j},(x^{j})_{j}\right)\in T^{\infty}_{o}\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})^{\infty}\times{\mathrm{C}}([0,T];{\mathbb{R}}^{d})
,
from which we deduce that

with

being a subdivision of
[0,T]
of step-size

(4.59)

\max_{k=1,\cdots,N(n)}\left|t_{k}^{(n)}-t_{k-1}^{(n)}\right|\leq n^{-3},

(the rationale for this choice, which implies in particular that
N(n)
grows at least like
n^{3}
with
n
, will become clear later in the proof), and
H^{(n),i}_{k}
being as in

[
58
, Lemma 4.3.2]
, i.e.,

(4.60)

H^{(n),i}_{k}=h^{(n),i}_{t_{k}^{(n)}}\quad\textrm{\rm with}\quad h^{(n),i}_{s}:=\sqrt{2\varsigma^{i}}\,n\int_{0}^{T}\varrho\left(n(s-r)\right)\eta_{n}\left({\rm D}u_{r}\left(\mu_{r}^{\infty},\xi_{r}^{i}\right)\right)\,\mathrm{d}r,\quad s\in[0,T],

where
\varrho

is a a smooth density on
{\mathbb{R}}
supported on the interval

[0,1]
and, for any
n\geq 1
,

\eta_{n}
is a bounded function, equal to the identity on the hypercube
[-n,n]^{d}
and satisfying the growth property
|\eta_{n}(x)|\leq|x|
for any
x\in{\mathbb{R}}^{d}
.
By using the definition of
\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

, we obtain

{\mathcal{I}}_{t}^{t_{0},(n)}\circ\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

and

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-3.8889pt}{-3.41666pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}

, we also notice that

H^{(n),i}_{k}\circ\hbox to10.72pt{\vbox to10.72pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 5.3582pt\lower-5.3582pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{{{}}}{{}}{}{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{}{}{}{}{{}\lxSVG@stroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 7.14 0 C 7.14 3.94 3.94 7.14 0 7.14 C -3.94 7.14 -7.14 3.94 -7.14 0 C -7.14 -3.94 -3.94 -7.14 0 -7.14 C 3.94 -7.14 7.14 -3.94 7.14 0 Z M 0 0}{fill:none} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces

\lxSVG@closescope }}}
